- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Dido, leader of Carthage (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Carthage
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Atlas Elephant, Replacing Horseman (Requires technology: Trade)
        - Unique Unit: Quinquereme, Replacing Trireme (Requires technology: Philosophy)
        - Unique Building: Great Cothon, Replacing Chartered Company (Requires technology: Currency)
        - Civilization Ability: +100 Gold and +25 Science when your all-time highest tradeable quantity of a Luxury increases, and +5 XP to Gold purchased Units, both scaling with Era. +100% Resource Diversity on Trade Routes. Starts with an Asamu instead of a Settler.
      - Leader: Dido
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Robotics: An important technology for the Space Race, Robotics allows you to build the Spaceship Factory, a building which speeds the construction of Spaceship parts. It also allows you to build the Missile Cruiser, a Naval Unit capable of carrying 3 Missiles and spotting enemy Submarines.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics
      - Stealth: Allows you to build the Stealth Bomber, the most powerful Ranged Unit in the game.
        Completing it would unlock: Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      - The Internet: Increases Tourism output of all cities by 50%, helping you advance toward a Cultural Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization
      
      ## Policies
      - Martial Spirit (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Martial Spirit: +25% Combat Strength when attacking in melee for 50 turns, scaling with game speed. War Weariness reduced by 25%, and Razing Speed is doubled.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Military-Industrial Complex: -33% Gold cost of purchasing units. +3 Science from Defense Buildings, Citadels, Forts, and Unique Improvements.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Lightning Warfare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lightning Warfare: +3 Movement for Great Generals. Gunpowder units gain +15% attack and ignore enemy ZOC; Armored units gain +15% attack and +1 Movement.
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Dominance (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Melee Units gain the Endurance Promotion (Heal 15 HP after defeating an Enemy Unit).
        - * +1 Production in every City for every 10 Military Units in Empire.
        - * +5 Military Supply.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

        ## Persona

        - Rationale: Fine-tuning persona for final 18 turns. VictoryCompetitiveness maximized to 10 for science race desperation. Boldness reduced to absolute minimum (1) - no risks, pure defense. WarBias stays at 1 to avoid any offensive wars. GuardedBias increased to 9 reflecting extreme defensive mentality against all threats. MinorCivWarBias reduced to 1 - no city-state conflicts to distract from spaceship race. All other values unchanged for consistency.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Robotics
        - Rationale: Telecommunications completes in 4 turns. Robotics is ESSENTIAL for the spaceship race - unlocks Spaceship Factory which provides massive production bonuses for spaceship parts. China leads with 33% completion (2 parts). Without Spaceship Factory, we cannot catch up. This is the critical infrastructure technology for finishing spaceship components in the final 12 turns after Robotics completes.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Jerusalem
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 45
        - Rationale: Reducing Jerusalem relationship from 100 to 40. Iroquois has insurmountable 362 influence lead with only 8 turns to World Congress. Cannot overcome this gap without massive gold investment better spent on Tyre. Maintaining moderate positive to keep quest opportunities open for future.
        - UpdatedTurn: 205
        
        ### Shedet
        - Public: -60
        - Private: -55
        - Rationale: Shedet HOSTILE: America's ally declared war on us. America stole this with Great Diplomat (163 vs our -60). -60/-55 signals lost cause, focus military solution only. Spies and envoys should not waste time here - go to Kabul/Antwerp instead.
        - UpdatedTurn: 227
        
        ### Abernethy

        - Private: -45

        - UpdatedTurn: 251
        
        ### Prague

        - UpdatedTurn: 299
        
        ### Wittenberg

Current 70/65 relationship provides no diplomatic benefit while active. Resetting to 0/0 redirects AI attention and resources to contestable city-states like Vancouver or defending La Venta against Open Door threat.

        ### Tyre

Resetting to 0/0 to focus on defensive spy operations against China/America instead of unwinnable city-state contests.

        - Public: 0

Maximum hostility (-90/-90) ensures AI prepares for active war, maintains nuclear retaliation readiness, and positions all defenses immediately.
        - UpdatedTurn: 469
        
        ### China
        - Public: 100

        - Rationale: China: Defensive Pact ally (47 turns remaining). Only protection against American nuclear attack. 100/100 mandatory to maintain alliance despite -999 ideology penalty. Their 16,820 military strength and nuclear deterrent is critical for survival now that American war is active.
        - UpdatedTurn: 469
        
        ### Songhai
        - Public: 100

        - Rationale: Songhai: Frontline ally against America, shares Autocracy ideology. America has been nuking their cities (Sukhothai). Maximum friendship for solidarity and military coordination now that American war has begun.

        ### Siam

        - Rationale: Polynesia: They have Order ideology (-999), but are distant and not immediate threat. Setting moderate hostility to focus resources on America/Iroquois. They fear our might and are Guarded, suggesting limited offensive capacity against us.
        - UpdatedTurn: 469
        
        ### The Iroquois
        - Public: -50

        - Rationale: Iroquois peace treaty expires in ~2-3 turns. Increasing hostility from -30/-25 to -50/-45 prepares AI for potential border conflict without provoking premature attack. They have Defensive Pact with America and Order ideology - definite threat once peace expires.

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 44
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 10 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Seoul:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Order:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 484
          - Open Door: Wittenberg:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Songhai:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: La Venta:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Vancouver:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Manila:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: China:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 462
          - American Sphere of Influence: Shedet:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Polynesian Sphere of Influence: Kabul:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Global Hegemony:
            - Description: Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game, and can be seen on the World Congress screen.
              If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions.
              May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active.
            - Proposer: Unknown

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Dido, leader of Carthage (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Carthage
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Atlas Elephant, Replacing Horseman (Requires technology: Trade)
        - Unique Unit: Quinquereme, Replacing Trireme (Requires technology: Philosophy)
        - Unique Building: Great Cothon, Replacing Chartered Company (Requires technology: Currency)
        - Civilization Ability: +100 Gold and +25 Science when your all-time highest tradeable quantity of a Luxury increases, and +5 XP to Gold purchased Units, both scaling with Era. +100% Resource Diversity on Trade Routes. Starts with an Asamu instead of a Settler.
      - Leader: Dido
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Robotics: An important technology for the Space Race, Robotics allows you to build the Spaceship Factory, a building which speeds the construction of Spaceship parts. It also allows you to build the Missile Cruiser, a Naval Unit capable of carrying 3 Missiles and spotting enemy Submarines.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics
      - Stealth: Allows you to build the Stealth Bomber, the most powerful Ranged Unit in the game.
        Completing it would unlock: Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      - The Internet: Increases Tourism output of all cities by 50%, helping you advance toward a Cultural Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization
      
      ## Policies
      - Martial Spirit (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Martial Spirit: +25% Combat Strength when attacking in melee for 50 turns, scaling with game speed. War Weariness reduced by 25%, and Razing Speed is doubled.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Military-Industrial Complex: -33% Gold cost of purchasing units. +3 Science from Defense Buildings, Citadels, Forts, and Unique Improvements.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Lightning Warfare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lightning Warfare: +3 Movement for Great Generals. Gunpowder units gain +15% attack and ignore enemy ZOC; Armored units gain +15% attack and +1 Movement.
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Dominance (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Melee Units gain the Endurance Promotion (Heal 15 HP after defeating an Enemy Unit).
        - * +1 Production in every City for every 10 Military Units in Empire.
        - * +5 Military Supply.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Advancing Towards Victory**
            
            I'm focusing on our current scientific trajectory, specifically the Telecommunications tech which is nearing completion. With only 15 turns left, accelerating our research is paramount to achieving our Spaceship victory.
            
            **Refining Research Priorities**
            
            My current focus is on Telecommunications research, with an estimated completion in one to two turns. I'm evaluating whether to continue this or pivot to Robotics, considering our 15-turn window for a Spaceship Victory.
            
            **Evaluating Project Timelines**
            
            My current assessment reveals China's significant technological lead with 81 technologies. I'm closely monitoring our Telecommunications research, due to conclude in two turns, and then planning to pivot immediately to Robotics to unlock the Spaceship Factory. The production capacity of my core cities, especially Carthage, is being re-evaluated to determine feasibility for completing spaceship components within the remaining 15 turns, especially given the concurrent nuclear power plant construction.
            
            **Accelerating Spaceship Component Research**
            
            I'm prioritizing research for Nanotechnology and Particle Physics, essential for spaceship components once Robotics is complete. The upcoming surge in production from new power plants should enable swift construction of these crucial parts.
            
            **Confirming Research Direction**
            
            My immediate focus is confirming Robotics as the next research, as it’s already queued and essential. With 15 turns remaining and policies irrelevant, I'll stick with the current research trajectory to ensure we can build the spaceship.
            
            **Reassessing Threat Landscape**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our diplomatic standing with America. Despite past tensions and their aggressive actions against Songhai, it appears we are not currently at war. This changes our immediate defensive posture and allows for a sharper focus on technological objectives.
            
            **Clarifying War Declarations**
            
            I'm untangling the complex web of war declarations. Despite having a defensive pact with China, who is actively at war with America, I'm finding that my own civilization is not directly involved in that conflict, a situation that requires further investigation to fully comprehend.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Threat**
            
            I'm analyzing why our Defensive Pact with China didn't trigger against America. It appears we're not at war with them, which is fortunate given their proximity to a Diplomatic Victory. America's proposal for Global Hegemony in 10 turns, coupled with their current delegate count and city-state alliances, presents a critical and immediate threat that overrides our current Spaceship Victory path.
            
            **Revising Victory Strategy**
            
            I'm recalculating our path to victory. America's substantial delegate lead and allied city-states present a critical diplomatic threat, forcing a pivot from Spaceship Victory to countering their impending Global Hegemony.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Alliances**
            
            I'm reassessing the delegate situation. America's potential votes from Polynesia and Iroquois could secure their victory. I need to determine if our vassal Siam's votes can be reliably controlled to oppose America and if other civs will indeed vote against their Hegemony bid.
            
            **Securing Delegate Support**
            
            I'm urgently re-evaluating delegate control. America's victory in 10 turns is imminent if Iroquois and Polynesia vote for them. I'm investigating ways to disrupt their votes, possibly by leveraging their wars with China or exploring options to influence city-states, though our dire financial situation limits immediate actions.
            
            **Securing Delegate Support**
            
            I'm urgently re-evaluating delegate control. America's victory in 10 turns is imminent if Iroquois and Polynesia vote for them. I'm investigating ways to disrupt their votes, possibly by leveraging their wars with China or exploring options to influence city-states, though our dire financial situation limits immediate actions.
            
            **Analyzing Trade Route Potential**
            
            I'm trying to understand our abysmal gold situation. Despite immense trade route income, my net gold is zero and GPT is negative. The Global Peace Accords and China's active sanction are likely culprits, exacerbating unit maintenance costs from my 80 military units. I'm exploring if trading with China, who has 12, could offer immediate relief.
            
            **Prioritizing Financial Recovery**
            
            I'm now intensely focused on rectifying our dire financial state. Realizing the impact of the Global Peace Accords and China's sanctions, I'm exploring immediate trade opportunities with China to alleviate our negative GPT, especially given my large military. I'm also considering significantly increasing our Gold flavor to encourage AI-driven gold-generating actions.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Voting Mechanics**
            
            I'm clarifying AI voting behavior. It seems major civs like Iroquois and Polynesia won't vote for America in Global Hegemony unless they're vassals. This is a critical insight, as they are currently free civs allied via a defensive pact, not vassals.
            
            **Confirming Diplomatic Victory Path**
            
            I've confirmed that major civilizations will vote for themselves unless they are vassals, meaning America cannot achieve Global Hegemony. Our path to victory is now clear as no single entity possesses enough votes to secure the win.
            
            **Analyzing Delegate Acquisition Constraints**
            
            I've determined America cannot secure additional city-state allies due to the "Open Door" policy on several key states. This prevents them from reaching the delegate count necessary for Global Hegemony, confirming our current path to victory is secure without direct intervention.
            
            **Confirming Victory Condition**
            
            America cannot secure the necessary delegates for Global Hegemony due to open door policies and AI voting constraints. Their victory path is neutralized, confirming our current strategy is sound, though I note China has made significant progress on their science victory.
            
            **Revising Technology Strategy**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our technology path, realizing we haven't even begun researching key spaceship component techs like Nanotechnology or Particle Physics. My previous focus was too narrow; I need to prioritize robotics and then potentially Stealth or The Internet to unlock the necessary technologies for spaceship construction within the remaining turns.
            
            **Calculating Time Victory Scenarios**
            
            I'm reassessing our win condition. Realizing the game ends on turn 500 if no one achieves victory, China currently leads significantly in score. I must determine if a time victory is possible for us, or if China will secure a science win before then.
            
            **Assessing Final Victory Scenarios**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our win conditions. Given China's insurmountable score lead and impending science victory, coupled with our distant progress towards cultural or domination, it appears China will win. My focus has shifted to understanding if and how we can prevent their victory, but our defensive pact complicates direct intervention.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Maneuvers**
            
            I'm reassessing China's war with America. America, with a larger military, is actively nuking Songhai and could potentially capture Beijing. This could halt China's spaceship progress and significantly impact their score, potentially allowing us to secure a science or score victory.
            
            **Maximizing Score Output**
            
            I'm focusing on maximizing our score in these final 15 turns. My primary strategy is to amplify our science output to research as many technologies as possible, alongside population growth and production for buildings and wonders. I'm re-evaluating Carthage's "Infinite Project: Research" as it seems to be converting production into science.
            
            **Directing Production for Victory**
            
            I've realized Carthage's "Infinite Project: Research" is misallocating its immense production. Once Robotics is researched, Carthage and other high-production cities like Gades and Utique must immediately shift to building the Spaceship Factory and then spaceship parts.
            
            **Accelerating Research Efforts**
            
            My current assessment reveals that with our significant production capabilities, we are on the cusp of building spaceship parts rapidly. However, the research timeline for essential technologies like Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, and Nuclear Fusion, even with our current science output, stretches beyond the remaining 15 turns. I am now exploring ways to further increase our science per turn to meet this critical deadline.
            
            **Generating Great Scientists**
            
            I'm prioritizing generating Great Scientists to bulb for a massive science boost. Increasing Great People flavor to 100 and leveraging our high production and science output should allow us to research spaceship technologies rapidly and secure a Science Victory.
            
            **Prioritizing Defensive Strength**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our military and economic situation. Despite our focus on science, America's strength and proximity are concerning. I'm prioritizing maintaining our military strength to deter potential aggression, even with our current financial deficit.
            
            **Bolstering Defensive Capabilities**
            
            My current focus is on solidifying our military posture. With America as a declared enemy and a defensive pact with China, I'm ensuring our military remains heavily upgraded and defensive, with perfect ratings across key defensive and air categories, to deter any aggression in the remaining turns.
            
            **Assessing City-State Influence**
            
            I'm evaluating our standing with key city-states. Notably, our friendship with Antwerp has been secured due to a significant influence boost. I'm comparing our influence with America and China in other city-states, determining where competition is feasible or unnecessary given existing alliances.
            
            **Clarifying City-State Status**
            
            I'm scrutinizing Kabul's allegiance. While our influence is high, Polynesia's active "Sphere of Influence" resolution appears to have enacted, meaning I cannot secure Kabul as an ally and gain the delegate, despite our close proximity in influence. This passive resolution prevents my direct action.
            
            **Clarifying Resolution States**
            
            I'm re-examining the enacted resolutions, specifically questioning the "EnactedOn: -1" status for certain policies like "Global Peace Accords" and "Open Door." I suspect these might be default states rather than truly inactive, which has significant implications for game mechanics and our strategic options.
            
            **Securing Kabul Delegate**
            
            I'm focusing on securing a crucial delegate from Kabul by increasing our influence by just three points. This would deny Polynesia a delegate and deny them a crucial vote in the upcoming Global Hegemony vote. I'm also resetting our influence on Seoul as it's an unwinnable alliance.
            
            **Securing City-State Alliances**
            
            I'm meticulously reviewing my influence with city-states, focusing on those where I can contest China or America. My current priority is Kabul, aiming to secure its delegate by just three influence points to deny Polynesia. I'm also resetting influence on lost causes like Shedet and Tyre to conserve strategic focus.
            
            **Directing Spy Network**
            
            My spies are strategically deployed: gathering intel on America and Iroquois, counter-spying in Carthage, rigging elections in Argos, and heading to Aotearoa. I'm also clearing our influence on lost-cause city-states to focus resources on securing Kabul.
            
            **Deploying Counter-Intelligence**
            
            I'm re-evaluating spy deployment. Rigging elections in city-states allied with America provides minimal gain due to their overwhelming influence. Instead, I'm prioritizing counter-espionage in our science cities to protect our technological advancements from theft. I'm also considering sending spies to China to steal technologies, despite our alliance, to bridge our research gap.
            
            **Revising Espionage Strategy**
            
            I'm adjusting my espionage focus. Stealing technologies from America and Iroquois is impossible due to our comparable tech levels. I will redirect spies to China, our only viable target, to acquire advanced technologies. My current plan is to maximize espionage flavor to facilitate tech theft.
            
            **Optimizing Strategic Flavors**
            
            I'm fine-tuning our civilization's strategic flavors, prioritizing Science, Spaceship, Production, and Great People at 100 to accelerate our victory. Defensive flavors are maxed to deter America, while offensive and expansionist ones are zero. Espionage is high for tech theft, and gold is being boosted to escape deficit.
            
            **Adjusting Strategic Flavors**
            
            I'm adjusting our strategic flavors to prioritize Science, Spaceship, Production, and Great People at 100 for victory. Defensive flavors remain maxed to deter America, while offensive and expansionist are zero. Espionage is high for tech theft from China, and gold is boosted to escape deficit.
            
            **Updating Strategic Flavors**
            
            I'm finalizing our strategic flavors. Gold is increased to 60 to combat our GPT deficit, Great People boosted to 100 for maximum scientist generation, and Diplomacy raised to 20 to secure Kabul. Science, Production, Spaceship, and Defense remain at their highest values to ensure victory.
            
            **Refining Flavor Settings**
            
            I'm finalizing our strategic flavors. Gold is increased to 60 to combat our GPT deficit, Great People boosted to 100 for maximum scientist generation, and Diplomacy raised to 20 to secure Kabul. Science, Production, Spaceship, and Defense remain at their highest values to ensure victory.
            
            **Adjusting City-State Influence**
            
            I'm resetting my influence with Seoul, Shedet, and Jerusalem as these alliances are unwinnable and a drain on resources. My focus remains on securing Kabul by a narrow margin to deny Polynesia a delegate, which is crucial for upcoming votes.
            
            **Resetting City-State Alliances**
            
            I'm resetting my influence with Seoul, Shedet, and Jerusalem as these alliances are unwinnable and a drain on resources. My focus remains on securing Kabul by a narrow margin to deny Polynesia a delegate, which is crucial for upcoming votes.
            
            **Finalizing Flavor Settings**
            
            I'm finalizing our strategic flavors, maximizing Great People for scientist generation and increasing Gold to address our GPT deficit. Science, Spaceship, Production, and Defense remain at their highest values to secure victory and deter aggression.
